∎

Example 2.19
.

The symmetric monoidal category
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
of all
C^{*}
-algebras with the maximal tensor product constitutes a symmetric monoidal
E
-theory context in the sense of
2.14
and
2.16
. This follows from the fact, that
the maximal tensor product in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
preserves finite products, filtered colimits and exact sequences.

By comparing universal properties, the lax symmetric monoidal functor obtained in
Theorem

2.18
coincides with the
E
-theory functor introduced in
[
Bun24
]
,
[
BD24
]
. As verified in
[
Bun24
, Sec. 13]
, on the level of homotopy categories it recovers the classical
E
-theory of
[
CH90
]
,
[
Hig90b
]
,
[
GHT00
]
.

∎

3
E
-theory contexts associated to posets

In this section, we introduce the
E
-theory contexts in the chain (
1.3
), along with several additional ones, and describe their categorical properties. The general principle is to describe each of them as a subcategory of the preceding category. We then consider closure properties under categorical constructions, such as exact sequences or filtered colimits. In most cases the smaller category is a localization of the ambient one; and in this case it inherits relevant categorical properties – for example presentability.

3.1
Functor categories

Since
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is a symmetric monoidal category we have a
lax symmetric monoidal functor

{\mathbf{Fun}}(-,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}):{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}\ ,

(3.1)

where
{\mathbf{Cat}}
is equipped with the Cartesian monoidal structure.

Proposition 3.1
.

The functor in (
3.1
) is a lax symmetric monoidal functor to
E
-theory contexts.

Proof.

We verify the conditions from
2.14
.

Clearly, we have
{\mathbf{Fun}}(\{*\},C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\simeq C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\in{\mathbf{CAlg}}({\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathbf{Cat}}))
.
Furthermore, for every
P
in
{\mathbf{Cat}}
the category
{\mathbf{Fun}}(P,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
is pointed by the constant functor
to zero algebra.

If
f:P\to Q
is a morphism in
{\mathbf{Cat}}
, then

f^{*}:{\mathbf{Fun}}(Q,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(P,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})

is
E
-admissible since limits and colimits in the functor category are formed pointwise and are therefore preserved by
f^{*}
.

For
A
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(P,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
and
Q
in
{\mathbf{Cat}}
the tensor product

A\boxtimes-:{\mathbf{Fun}}(Q,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(P\times Q,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})

sends
B
in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(Q,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
to the functor

(p,q)\mapsto A(p)\otimes B(q)
.
Since limits and colimits in the functor category are formed pointwise and the maximal tensor product in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
preserves finite products, filtered colimits and exact sequences
we conclude that
A\boxtimes-
is
E
-admissible.
∎

Note that
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is a presentable category. It is
\aleph_{1}
-presentable, and its subcategory of
\aleph_{1}
-compact objects is precisely the subcategory of separable
C^{*}
-algebras.
In particular,
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
admits all limits and colimits.

Corollary 3.2
.

For every
P
in
{\mathbf{Cat}}

the functor category
{\mathbf{Fun}}(P,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
is presentable.

Consequently, the category
{\mathbf{Fun}}(P,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
has all limits and colimits.

3.2
P
-
C^{*}
-algebras

A poset
P
is called pointed if it admits a maximal element
\infty_{P}
.
We consider the full symmetric monoidal subcategory of pointed posets,
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}\subseteq{\mathbf{Cat}}
.
The notation
(*)
instead of
*
indicates that morphisms in
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}
are not required to preserve the maximal elements.

Assume that
P
is a pointed poset.

Definition 3.3
.

1.

A
P
-
C^{*}
-algebra is an object
A
of
{\mathbf{Fun}}(P,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})

such that for every
p,q
in
P
with
p\leq q
the map
A(p)\to A(q)
is an ideal inclusion.

2.

We let
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
denote the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{Fun}}(P,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
of
P
-
C^{*}
-algebras.

Remark 3.4
.

We let
I(A)
denote the frame of closed two-sided
*
-ideals in a
C^{*}
-algebra
A
with the inclusion relation.
An object of
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is a pair
(A,A(-):P\to I(A))
consisting of a
C^{*}
-algebra and a poset morphism such that
A(\infty_{P})=A
.
We call
A
the underlying algebra.
A morphism of
P
-
C^{*}
-algebras
(A,A(-))\to(B,B(-))
is then morphism
f:A\to B
of
C^{*}
-algebras such that

f(A(p))\subseteq B(p)
for all
p
in
P
.

Proposition 3.5
.

For every pointed poset
P
we have a left Bousfield localization

L:{\mathbf{Fun}}(P,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\leftrightarrows PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathrm{incl}\ .

(3.2)

The category
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is presentable, and the inclusion
\mathrm{incl}
is
E
-admissible.

Proof.

We first construct the functor
L
and provide the unit and counit of the adjunction.
If
A
is in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(P,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
, then
we set
L(A)(\infty_{P}):=A(\infty_{P})
. For every
p
in
P

we furthermore define
L(A)(p)
as the ideal generated by the image of
A(p)\to A(\infty_{P})
.
We extend
L
to a functor by defining it on morphisms in the canonical way.

The unit and counit of the adjunction are given by the canonical morphism

A\to\mathrm{incl}\circ L(A)
and the identity
L\circ\mathrm{incl}(A)=A
.
The triangle identities are straightforward to check.

We now check that
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is closed in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(P,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
under filtered colimits. In

{\mathbf{Fun}}(P,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
they are calculated pointwise. We employ the fact that filtered colimits in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
preserve ideal inclusions (see
7.1
).

Since
\mathrm{incl}
preserves kernels, it remains to show that
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is closed in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(P,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
under quotients. We consider a pushout

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C}

in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(P,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})

and assume that
A
and
B
belong to
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. We can assume that
A\cong{\mathtt{ker}}(B\to C)
without changing the quotient. We must show that

C
also belongs to
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. Since quotients in the functor category are formed pointwise,
we have
C(p)\cong B(p)/A(p)
for every
p
in
P
. Then,

B(p)/A(p)\cong(B(p)+A(\infty_{P}))/A(\infty_{P})

is an ideal in
C(\infty_{P})
.

Since
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is an accessible left Bousfield localization of a presentable category, it is itself presentable.
∎

Remark 3.6
.

One can check that the Bousfield localization (
3.2
) is symmetric monoidal, and that
\mathrm{incl}
and
L
are symmetric monoidal.
∎

Proposition 3.7
.

We have a lax symmetric monoidal functor

(-)C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}

(3.3)

to
E
-theory contexts, with the structure of a subfunctor of
{\mathbf{Fun}}(-,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})_{|\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}}
.

Proof.

If
f:P\to Q
is a morphism in
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}
, then

f^{*}
sends
A
in
QC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to the functor

p\mapsto A(f(p))
, which again belongs to
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

For
A
in
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
and
B
in
QC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
one checks using
7.2
that
the tensor product

A\boxtimes B
belongs to
(P\times Q)C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

By
3.5
the category
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is closed in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(P,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
under finite products and filtered colimits, and
the inclusion
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(P,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
is
E
-admissible.

It therefore follows from
3.1
that
(-)C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is a lax symmetric monoidal functor to
E
-theory contexts, and that the inclusion is a natural transformation between lax symmetric monoidal functors to
E
-theory contexts
(-)C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(-,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})_{|\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}}
.
∎

3.3
Left exact
P
-
C^{*}
-algebras

A morphism between posets is called partially left-exact if it preserves all non-empty finite meets.
So ”partially” means that we do not require the preservation of the maximal element.
We let
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{ple}}
denote the wide subcategory of
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}
consisting of partially left-exact morphisms. A morphism in
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{ple}}
is
left-exact if it also preserves empty meets, i.e., maximal elements.

Assume that
P
is a pointed poset.

Definition 3.8
.

1.

A
P
-
C^{*}
-algebra is called left-exact if the functor
A:P\to I(A(\infty_{P}))
is left-exact.

2.

We let
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
denote the full subcategory of
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
consisting of left-exact
P
-
C^{*}
-algebras.

Proposition 3.9
.

For every pointed poset
P
we have a left Bousfield localization

L:PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\leftrightarrows P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathrm{incl}\ .

(3.4)

The category
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is presentable, and the inclusion
\mathrm{incl}
is
E
-admissible.

Proof.

We first show that
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is closed in
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
under filtered colimits.
We use that filtered colimits of exact sequences are exact (see
7.1
). Let
(A_{i})_{i\in I}
be a filtered family in
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
with colimit
A
in
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
We must show that
A
is again left exact. Let
(p_{f})_{f\in F}
be a finite family in
P
such that
\bigwedge_{f\in F}p_{f}
exists. Then we have the system of exact sequences

0\to\bigcap_{f\in F}A_{i}(p_{f})\to A_{i}(p)\to A_{i}(p)/A_{i}(\bigwedge_{f\in F}p_{f})\to 0

since the
A_{i}
are left-exact. Hence

0\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}\bigcap_{f\in F}A_{i}(p_{f})\to A(p)\to A(p)/A(\bigwedge_{f\in F}p_{f})\to 0\ .

We now use that

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}\bigcap_{f\in F}A_{i}(p_{f})\cong\bigcap_{f\in F}A(p_{f})\ .

To see this, we note that we can realize the filtered colimit inside
A
. Then
the relation
\subseteq
is clear. Every element of
\bigcap_{f\in F}A(p_{f})
can be approximated by products

\prod_{f\in F}a_{f}
with
a_{f}\in A(p_{f})
.
For fixed
f
the element
a_{f}
can be approximated by the image of an element
a_{i,f}
in
A_{i}(p_{f})
.
Since
F
is finite, we can take a common
i
and then

\prod_{f\in F}a_{i,f}\in\bigcap_{f\in F}A_{i}(p_{f})
.
This shows that
a
belongs to
\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}\bigcap_{f\in F}A_{i}(p_{f})
.
We conclude that

A(\bigwedge_{f\in F}p_{f})\cong\bigcap_{f\in F}A(p_{f})\ .

Note that limits in
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
are created in
{\mathbf{Fun}}(P,C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})
and are therefore taken pointwise.
Since left-exactness is a condition which can be phrased in terms of limits involving the values of the functors,
this condition is preserved under limits. Consequently,
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is closed in
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
under limits.

We can now apply the adjoint functor theorem and obtain the existence of the left-adjoint
L
.
Since
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is an accessible left Bousfield localization
of a presentable category it is itself presentable.

In order to show that
\mathrm{incl}
is
E
-admissibe it remains to show that it preserves finite products and exact sequences.
Being a right adjoint, it preserves finite products and kernels; we must show that it also preserves quotients. To this end,
it suffices to show that
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is closed in
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
under quotients. We consider a push-out

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C}

in
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
and assume that
A
and
B
are in
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. We must show that then,
C
is also in
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. We can replace
A
by the kernel of
B\to C
.
Let
(p_{f})_{f\in F}
be a finite family in
P
such that
\bigwedge_{f\in F}p_{f}
exists.
Then

\displaystyle\bigcap_{f\in F}C(p_{f})=\bigcap_{f\in F}\frac{B(p_{f})}{A(p_{f})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}\frac{\bigcap_{f\in F}B(p_{f})}{\bigcap_{f\in F}A(p_{f})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!!}}{{=}}\frac{B(\bigwedge_{f\in F}p_{f})}{A(\bigwedge_{f\in F}p_{f})}=C(\bigwedge_{f\in F}p_{f})\ ,

where the equality marked by
!
is due to
7.8
and
!!
follows from the left-exactness of
A
and
B
.
∎

Remark 3.10
.

One can check that the left Bousfield localization (
3.4
) and
\mathrm{incl}
are symmetric monoidal.
∎

Proposition 3.11
.

We have a lax symmetric monoidal functor

(-)^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{ple}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}

(3.5)

to
E
-theory contexts, with the structure of a subfunctor of
(-)C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{|\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{ple}}}
.

Proof.

If
f:P\to Q
is a morphism in
\mathbf{Poset}^{\mathrm{ple}}_{(*)}
, then

f^{*}
sends
A
in
Q^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to an object of

P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
since
f
preserves non-empty meets.

For
A
in
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
and
B
in
Q^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
one checks using
7.6
that
the tensor product

A\boxtimes B
belongs to
(P\times Q)^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

By
3.9
the category
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is closed in
PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
under finite products and filtered colimits,
and the inclusion
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is
E
-admissible.

It therefore follows from
3.7
that
(-)^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is a lax symmetric monoidal functor to
E
-theory contexts, and that the inclusion
(-)^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to(-)C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{|\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{ple}}}
is a natural transformation between lax symmetric monoidal functors to
E
-theory contexts.
∎

Remark 3.12
.

If
f:P\to Q
in
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}
is partially left-exact, then
f(\infty_{P})
is not necessarily a maximal element of
Q
.
For
A
in
Q^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
the underlying
C^{*}
-algebra of
f^{*}A
is therefore
A(f(\infty_{P}))
, which is an ideal in
A
. However, if
f
is left-exact, then
f^{*}

preserves the underlying
C^{*}
-algebras.
∎

3.4
Regular
P
-
C^{*}
-algebras

A map between posets is a called preframe morphism if it preserves finite meets and filtered joins.
If it only preserves non-empty finite meets, then we add the prefix ”partial”. We let
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pprfr}}
denote the symmetric monoidal category of pointed posets and partial
preframe morphisms.

Let
P
be a pointed poset.

Definition 3.13
.

1.

A left exact
P
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A
is called regular if the functor
A:P\to I(A)
is a preframe morphism.

2.

We let
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
denote the full subcategory of
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
consisting of regular
P
-
C^{*}
-algebras.

Proposition 3.14
.

The subcategory
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
of
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is closed under finite products, filtered colimits, kernels, and quotients, and
the inclusion
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is
E
-admissible.

Proof.

Since regularity is formulated as a condition involving filtered colimits of the evaluations, and since
finite products, filtered colimits, and quotients in
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
are formed pointwise,
we can conclude that the subcategory
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
of
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is closed under finite products, filtered colimits, and quotients.
For the kernels, we consider a pullback

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C}

in
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
and assume that

B
and
C
belong to
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
We must show that then,
A
also belongs to
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. We will use the distributivity law in the frame
I(B)
.
Let
(p_{i})_{\in I}
be a filtered family in
P
such that
p:=\bigvee_{i\in I}p_{i}
exists.
Then
A(p_{i})
for every
i
in
I
is also an ideal in
B
, and

A(p)=A\cap B(p)=A\cap\bigvee_{i\in I}B(p_{i})=\bigvee_{i\in I}(A\cap B(p_{i}))=\bigvee_{i\in I}A(p_{i})\ .

∎

Proposition 3.15
.

We have a lax symmetric monoidal functor

(-)^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pprfr}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}

(3.6)

to
E
-theory contexts, with the structure of a subfunctor of
(-)^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{|\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pprfr}}}
.

Proof.

If
f:P\to Q
is a morphism in
\mathbf{Poset}^{\mathrm{pprfr}}_{(*)}
, then

f^{*}
sends
A
in
Q^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to an object of
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
since
f
preserves non-empty meets and filtered joins.

For
A
in
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
and
B
in
Q^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
one checks using
7.7
that
the tensor product

A\boxtimes B
belongs to
(P\times Q)^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

By
3.14
the category
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is closed in
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
under finite products, and filtered colimits, and the
inclusion
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is
E
-admissible.

It therefore follows from
3.11
that
(-)^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is a lax symmetric monoidal functor to
E
-theory contexts, and that the inclusion
(-)^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to(-)^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{|\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pprfr}}}
is a natural transformation between lax symmetric monoidal functors to
E
-theory contexts.
∎

Under additional conditions on the poset
P
we can present the category of regular
P
-
C^{*}
-algebras as a right Bousfield localization of the category of left-exact ones and show that
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is presentable. In the following we explain the details.

Let
P
be a poset. We recall the following definitions from the theory of posets (see
[
Leh26
, Sec. 3]
for more background).

1.

For
p,q
in
P
we say that
p\ll q
(way below) if for every filtered subset
R
in
P
with
q=\bigvee R
there exists
r
in
R
such that
p\leq r
.

2.

P
is called a preframe if it admits finite meets and all filtered joins, and if meets distribute over filtered joins.

3.

A preframe
P
is locally compact if for every
q
in
P
we have

q=\bigvee\{p\in P\mid p\ll q\}\ .

4.

A preframe
P
is stably locally compact if
p\ll q
and
p\ll r
imply
p\ll q\wedge r
.

Let
P
be a poset

Definition 3.16
.

We define a functor

R:PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
given on objects by

A\mapsto\left(q\mapsto R(A)(q):=\bigvee_{p\ll q}A(p)\right)

for all
q
in
P
, and on morphisms in the canonical manner.

We have a natural transformation

r:R\to{\mathtt{id}}:PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to PC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}

induced by the obvious inclusions
R(A)(q)\subseteq A(q)
of ideals for all
q
in
P
.
The functor
R
in (
3.7
) below is the restriction of the functor from
3.16

to
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
, and implicitly we claim that it then takes values in
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

Theorem 3.17
.

We assume that
P
is a stably locally compact preframe.

1.

We have a right Bousfield localization

\mathrm{incl}:P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\leftrightarrows P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:R\ .

(3.7)

2.

The functor
R
in (
3.7
) is
E
-admissible.

3.

The category

P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is presentable.

Proof.

We start by showing that
R
sends
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
Let
A
be in
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. We first show that
R(A)
is again left-exact.
Let
p,q
be in
P
.
Then we calculate

\displaystyle R(A)(p\wedge q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigvee_{r\ll p\wedge q}A(r)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle!}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\bigvee_{r\ll p\land s\ll q}A(r)\wedge A(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigvee_{r\ll p}A(r)\wedge\bigvee_{s\ll q}A(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R(A)(p)\wedge R(A)(q)

where we used the facts that
P
is stably locally compact and
A
is left-exact at the equality marked by
!
. In order to see that
R(A)
is regular, we calculate

\displaystyle\bigvee_{p\ll q}R(A)(p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigvee_{p\ll q}\bigvee_{r\ll p}A(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigvee_{r\ll q}A(r)

since
(r)_{\exists p\mid r\ll p\ll q}
is cofinal in
(r)_{r\ll q}
.

We now provide the unit and the counit of the adjunction (
3.7
).
We have a natural transformation

r:\mathrm{incl}\circ R\to{\mathtt{id}}
induced by the inclusions
R(A)(p)\to A(p)
.
We furthermore have an isomorphism

R\circ\mathrm{incl}\cong{\mathtt{id}}
since in this case the inclusions are equalities.

Since
R
is a right-adjoint, it preserves finite products and kernels.
We next show that
R
preserves filtered colimits and quotients.
Filtered colimits in
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
and
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
are calculated pointwise.
Since
R(A)(p)
is given by a colimit of values of
A
, and since colimits commute with colimits,
we conclude that
R
commutes with filtered colimits.

We consider a push-out diagram

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C}

in
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
We can assume that
A
is the kernel of
B\to C
.
We must show that

\textstyle{R(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R(C)}

is again a push-out diagram.
Since quotients in
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
and
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
are calculated pointwise,
it suffices to show that for every
q
in
P

\textstyle{R(A)(q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R(B)(q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R(C)(q)}

is a push-out in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. Let
c
be in
R(C)(q)=\bigvee_{p\ll q}C(p)
.
Then then we can choose
c^{\prime}
in
\bigcup_{p\ll q}C(p)
approximating
c
.
We can then find a lift
b^{\prime}
in
\bigcup_{p\ll q}B(p)
of
c^{\prime}
.
It follows that
R(B)(q)\to R(C)(q)
has a dense range, and is therefore surjective.

Assume now that
b
in
R(B)(q)=\bigvee_{p\ll q}B(p)

is sent to zero in
R(C)(q)
. Then
b
in
A
.
We can approximate
b
by elements
b^{\prime}
in
\bigcup_{p\ll q}B(p)
.
Then also
ub^{\prime}
approximates
b
for suitable members
u
of an approximate unit of
A
.
Then
ub^{\prime}\in A(p)
for some
p\ll q
.
It follows that
b\in R(A)(q)
.

We finally show that
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is presentable. By
3.9
there exists

\kappa
such that
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is
\kappa
-presentable. An object of
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}

which is
\kappa
-compact in
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is also
\kappa
-compact in
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
since
\mathrm{incl}
preserves filtered colimits, so in particular
\kappa
-filtered ones.
Since
R
preserves filtered colimits,
\mathrm{incl}
preserves
\kappa
-compact objects. Hence the

\kappa
-compact objects in
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
are precisely the
\kappa
-compact objects in
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
which are in addition regular. If
A
is in
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
, then
\mathrm{incl}A\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i}A_{i}
in
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
for a system of
\kappa
-compact objects
A_{i}
in
P^{\mathrm{le}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
Then also
A\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i}R(A_{i})
in
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. It remains to see that

R
preserves
\kappa
-compact objects. Using the explicit description of
R
, if we take

\kappa

bigger than the cardinality of
P
, then this is the case.
∎

3.5
Almost continuous
P
-
C^{*}
-algebras

A morphism between posets is called an almost partial frame morphism if it preserves all non-empty finite meets and all non-empty joins. The word ”almost” thus indicates that we do not require the preservation of the minimal element. If it preserves all finite meets, then it is called an almost frame morphism. We let
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pafr}}
denote the category of posets and partial almost frame morphisms.

Let
P
be a pointed poset.

Definition 3.18
.

1.

A regular
P
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A
is called almost continuous if the functor
A:P\to I(A)
is an almost frame morphism.

2.

We let
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
denote the full subcategory of
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
consisting of almost continuous
P
-
C^{*}
-algebras.

Let
P
be a pointed poset.

Lemma 3.19
.

The subcategory
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is closed in
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
under finite products, filtered colimits, kernels, and quotients, and the inclusion

P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is
E
-admissible.

Proof.

Let
(A_{i})_{i\in I}
be a filtered system in
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
and
A:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}A_{i}
in
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. We must show that
A
is again almost continuous.
Let
(p_{k})_{k\in K}
be a family in
P
whose join exists. It is clear, that

\bigvee_{k\in K}A(p_{k})\subseteq A(\bigvee_{k\in K}p_{k})

and we must show the converse inclusion.
Consider
a
in
A(\bigvee_{k\in K}p_{k})
. Then

a
can be approximated by images of elements in
A_{i}(\bigvee_{k}p_{k})
.
The latter are in
\bigvee_{k\in K}A_{i}(p_{k})
, and hence the approximants belong to
\bigvee_{k\in K}A(p_{k})
. Consequently,

a
is in
\bigvee_{k\in K}A(p_{k})
.

Since we already know that quotients in
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
are formed pointwise and the additional condition for being in
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is the preservation of finite non-empty joins, i.e., some finite colimits,
it is clear that
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is closed in
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
under
quotients.

The closedness of
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
in
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
under finite products is clear.

We now consider kernels.
Assume that

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C}

is a pullback in
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}

such that

B
belongs to
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. For the present purpose, we could assume that
C
belongs to
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
, but we do not need to do this. Let
(p_{i})_{i\in I}
be a family in
P
such that
p:=\bigvee_{i\in I}p_{i}
exists.
Then
A(p_{i})
for every
i
in
I
is also an ideal in
B
, and

A(p)=A\cap B(p)=A\cap\bigvee_{i\in I}B(p_{i})=\bigvee_{i\in I}(A\cap B(p_{i}))=\bigvee_{i\in I}A(p_{i})\ ,

where we use that
I(A)
is a frame and hence distributive.
∎

Proposition 3.20
.

We have a functor

(-)^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pafr}}\to\mathcal{EC}

(3.8)

to
E
-theory contexts, with the structure of a subfunctor of
(-)^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{|\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pafr}}}
.

Proof.

Note that we do not claim that this functor is lax symmetric monoidal.

If
f:P\to Q
is a morphism in
\mathbf{Poset}^{\mathrm{pac}}_{(*)}
, then

f^{*}
sends
A
in
Q^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to an object of
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
since
f
preserves all non-empty finite meets and non-empty joins.

For
A
in
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
and
B
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
one checks using
7.7
that
the tensor product

A\boxtimes B
belongs to
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
The functor
{\mathcal{A}}
takes values in
{\mathbf{Mod}}_{C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}}({\mathbf{Cat}})
, so, in particular, also in
{\mathbf{Mod}}_{\mathcal{N}}({\mathbf{Cat}})
.

By
3.19

the category
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is closed in
P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
under finite products, and filtered colimits, and
the inclusion
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to P^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is
E
-admissible. It therefore follows from
3.15
that
(-)^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is a functor to
E
-theory contexts, and that the inclusion

(-)^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to(-)^{\mathrm{reg}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{|\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pafr}}}
is a natural transformation between functors to
E
-theory contexts.
∎

3.6
Continuous
P
-
C^{*}
-algebras

A morphism between posets is called a partial frame morphism if it preserves non-empty finite meets and all joins.
We let
\mathbf{Poset}^{\mathrm{pfr}}_{(*)}
denote the category of pointed posets and partial frame morphisms.

Example 3.21
.

Let
X
be a topological space and
U
be an open subset. Then, the inclusion
\mathrm{Open}(U)\to\mathrm{Open}(X)
is a partial frame morphism. It corresponds to the partially defined map
X\supseteq U\to U
. This is the motivation for the word partial. Partially defined maps
between locally compact Hausdorff spaces occur, for example, in the description of the Gelfand dual of a map between commutative
C^{*}
-algebras.
∎

Let
P
be a pointed poset.

Definition 3.22
.

1.

An almost continuous
P
-
C^{*}
-algebra
A
is called continuous if the functor
A:P\to I(A)
is a frame morphism.

2.

We let
P^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
denote the full subcategory of
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
consisting of continuous
P
-
C^{*}
-algebras.

Compared with almost continuity, the only new condition is that
A(0_{P})=0
.

Lemma 3.23
.

The subcategory
P^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is closed in
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
under finite products, filtered colimits, kernels, and quotients, and the inclusion

P^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is
E
-admissible.

Proof.

The assertions are straightforward to check.
∎

Proposition 3.24
.

We have a functor

(-)^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pfr}}\to\mathcal{EC}

(3.9)

to
E
-theory contexts with the structure of a subfunctor of
(-)^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{|\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pfr}}}
.

Proof.

If
f:P\to Q
is a morphism in
\mathbf{Poset}^{\mathrm{pfr}}_{(*)}
, then

f^{*}
sends
A
in
Q^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to
P^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
since
f
preserves non-empty meets and all joins.

For
A
in
P^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
and
B
in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
,
the tensor product

A\boxtimes B
belongs to
P^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

By
3.23
the category
P^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is closed in
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
under finite products and filtered colimits, and
the inclusion
P^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is
E
-admissible. It therefore follows from
3.20
that
(-)^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is a functor to
E
-theory contexts, and that the inclusion

(-)^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to(-)^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}_{|\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pfr}}}

is a natural transformation between functors to
E
-theory contexts.
∎

3.7
The
E
-theory functor for locales and topological spaces

Recall that a frame is a poset, which admits finite meets and all joins, and in which meets distribute over joins. A morphism between frames is a morphism of posets which preserves finite meets and all joins.
The category of locales is defined by

\mathbf{Locale}:=\mathbf{Frame}^{\mathrm{op}}\ .

Example 3.25
.

The typical example of a frame is the poset of open subsets of a topological space
\mathrm{Open}(X)
. A continuous map
f:X\to Y
between topological spaces
induces a frame morphism
f^{-1}:\mathrm{Open}(Y)\to\mathrm{Open}(X)
. For us, the motivation to introduce locales is the desire to
use a uniform symbolic language for topological spaces and frames.
∎

For a locale
X
we let
\mathcal{P}(X)
denote the corresponding poset.

Definition 3.26
.

We define the functor

(-)C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:=(\mathcal{P}(-))^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathbf{Locale}\to\mathcal{EC}\ ,

where for a map of locales

f:X\to Y
, we use the notation

f_{!}:=\mathcal{P}(f)^{*}:XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to YC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ .

Definition 3.27
.

We define the
E
-theory functor for locales by

E:\mathbf{Locale}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(-)C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{EC}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{E}}}{{\to}}\mathbf{Pr}^{L}_{\mathrm{st}}\ ,\quad X\mapsto E(X):=E(XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ ,\quad f\mapsto f_{!}:=E(f_{!})\ .

We have a functor
{\mathbf{Top}}\to\mathbf{Locale}
which sends a topological space
X
to the locale
\mathrm{Open}(X)
and a map
f:X\to Y

of topological spaces to the map of frames
f^{-1}:\mathrm{Open}(Y)\to\mathrm{Open}(X)
.

Definition 3.28
.

For a topological space
X
we write

E(X):=\mathrm{Open}(X)^{c}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ .

and for a morphism

f:X\to Y
between topological spaces, we set

f_{!}:E(X)\to E(Y)
.

Example 3.29
.

A primitive ideal in a
C^{*}
-algebra
A
is the kernel of an irreducible representation

A\to B(H)
, where
B(H)
is the
C^{*}
-algebra of bounded operators on some Hilbert space
H
. We let
\mathrm{Prim}(A)
denote the topological space of primitive ideals, which is equipped with the
Jacobson topology
[
Dix83
, 3.1.1]
. The closed subsets of
\mathrm{Prim}(A)
in this topology are the sets
\{p\in\mathrm{Prim}(A)\mid I\subseteq p\}

for the closed ideals
I
of
A
. It follows that
there is an isomorphism of frames,
\mathrm{Open}(\mathrm{Prim}(A))\cong I(A)
which sends an open
set
U
to the closed ideal
A(U):=\bigcap_{p\in\mathrm{Prim}(A)\setminus U}p
. We conclude that

\mathrm{Prim}(A)C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\simeq I(A)^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ ,

where
\mathrm{Prim}(A)
on the left-hand side is considered as a topological space or locale, and
I(A)
on the right-hand side is a pointed poset.

∎

4
Adjunctions, localization and cosheaves

4.1
Continuous and almost continuous
P
-
C^{*}
-algebras

Recall that a morphism
f:P\to Q
between posets is a partial almost frame morphism if it preserves all non-empty
finite meets and all non-empty finite joins. It is a partial frame morphism, if it, in addition, preserves the empty join.
By
3.24
we have the functor
(-)^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to
E
-theory contexts defined on
the category
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pfr}}
of pointed posets and partial frame morphisms.
In the following, we extend its functoriality to the category
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pafr}}
of pointed posets and
partial almost frame morphisms.

Proposition 4.1
.

1.

For every
P
in
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}
having a minimal element, we have a left Bousfield localization

L:P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\leftrightarrows P^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathrm{incl}\ .

(4.1)

2.

For all
P,Q
in
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}
and every minimal element preserving morphism
f:P\to Q
in
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pfr}}
the square

\textstyle{Q^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{incl}}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}
\textstyle{Q^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}
\textstyle{P^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{incl}}
\textstyle{P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}}

(4.2)

is horizontally left-adjoinable.

3.

The functors
L
and
\mathrm{incl}
in (
4.1
) are morphisms of
E
-theory contexts.

Proof.

Let
0_{P}
denote the minimal element in
P
.
The functor
L
sends
A
in
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to the functor

L(A):p\mapsto A(p)/A(0_{P})\ .

One checks that it belongs to
P^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
The unit of the left Bousfield localization is induced by the canonical projections
A(p)\to A(p)/A(0_{P})
for all
p
in
P
.
If
A
is in
P^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
, then
A(0_{P})=0
and the counit is the isomorphism
L(\mathrm{incl}(A))\cong A
.
The triangle identities are straightforward to check. This shows Assertion
1
.

Since
f
in Assertion
2
preserves minimal elements, we have

(f^{*}A)(p)/(f^{*}A)(0_{P})\cong A(f(p))/A(f(0_{P}))\cong L(f^{*}A)(p)\ .

This implies that the square in Assertion
2
is horizontally left adjointable.

We finally show Assertion
3
. In order to show that
L
is
E
-admissible
we use the fact that finite products, and filtered colimits, and exact sequences in
P^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
and
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
are formed pointwise.
If
(A_{i})_{i\in I}
is a filtered system in
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
, then
for every
p
in
P
the colimit of the exact sequences

0\to A_{i}(0_{P})\to A_{i}(p)\to A_{i}(p)/A_{i}(0_{P})\to 0

is the exact sequence

0\to A(0_{P})\to A(p)\to A(p)/A(0_{P})\to 0\ .

Looking at the third terms, we conclude that

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}L(A)\cong L(\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}A_{i})\ .

The argument for finite products is similar.
In order to see that
L
preserves exact sequences, assume that

0\to A\to B\to C\to 0

is exact in
P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
Then for
p
in
P
we get a web of vertical exact sequences

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 142.07967pt\raise-126.94443pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

Since the upper two horizontal sequences are exact by assumption, the lower horizontal sequence is also exact.

In order to see that
L
is a morphism of
\mathcal{N}
-modules we use that for
N
in
\mathcal{N}
and
p
in
P
we have the relations

L(A)(p)\otimes N\cong(A(p)/A(0_{P}))\otimes N\cong(A(p)\otimes N)/(A(0_{P})\otimes N)\cong L(A\otimes N)(p)\ .

It is straightforward to see that
\mathrm{incl}
in (
4.1
) is a morphism of
E
-theory contexts.
∎

Proposition 4.2
.

We have an extension of functors

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 22.20897pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-22.20897pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{Poset}^{\mathrm{pfr}}_{(*)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.53793pt\raise-21.89778pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{incl}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.20099pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.86012pt\raise 6.54709pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.79709pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(-)^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 118.28667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 67.24782pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 118.28667pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{EC}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-32.93448pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 46.20897pt\raise-32.93448pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{Poset}^{\mathrm{pafr}}_{(*)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 88.30853pt\raise-23.01433pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.79709pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(-)^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 118.50784pt\raise-5.55554pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 125.30057pt\raise-32.93448pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

Proof.

The extension is given as follows:

1.

objects: It sends
P
in
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pafr}}
to
P^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.

2.

morphisms: It sends
f:P\to Q
in
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pafr}}
to the composition

Q^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f^{*}}}{{\to}}P^{\mathrm{ac}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle L}}{{\to}}P^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ .

3.

Compositions: Let
g:Q\to R
be a second morphism in
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pafr}}
. We add subscripts to the symbols for the localizations
L
.
We have a natural transformation

L_{P}f^{*}g^{*}\simeq L_{P}L_{P}f^{*}g^{*}\to L_{P}f^{*}L_{Q}g^{*}

induced by the Beck-Chevalley map
L_{P}f^{*}\to f^{*}L_{Q}
for
the square (
4.2
). The latter might not be an isomorphism since

f
may not preserve the minimal elements. But one checks that the whole composition is an isomorphism.
The pentagon relation for the composition of three morphisms is satisfied.

∎

4.2
Adjunctions between frames

In this section we collect some facts about adjunctions between frames for later use.
We suggest to consult
[
Leh26
, Sec. 2-3]
for a condensed presentationof most of the definitions and facts below.

Recall that an adjunction in
\mathbf{Poset}
is a property of a pair of functors.
Assume that
f:P\to Q
is a morphism of posets.

Lemma 4.3
.

If
P
is a frame and
f
is a frame morphism, then we have an adjunction

f:P\leftrightarrows Q:f_{\sharp}

(4.3)

in
\mathbf{Poset}^{\mathrm{le}}
. If
f
is surjective, then
{\mathtt{id}}=f_{\sharp}f
. If
f
is injective, then

f_{\sharp}f={\mathtt{id}}
.

Proof.

Since
P
is a frame
we can set

f_{\sharp}(q):=\bigvee_{\{p\in P\mid f(p)\leq q\}}p

and observe that

ff_{\sharp}(q)\leq q
and
p\leq f_{\sharp}f(p)
.
The additional statements are straightforward.
∎

Definition 4.4
.

A frame morphism
f:P\to Q
is called perfect if
f_{\sharp}
preserves filtered joins.

Let
\mathbf{Poset}^{\mathrm{prfr}}
be the subcategory of preframe morphisms.

Corollary 4.5
.

If
f:P\to Q
is perfect, then we have an adjunction

f:P\leftrightarrows Q:f_{\sharp}

(4.4)

in
\mathbf{Poset}^{\mathrm{prfr}}
.

Recall that the category of locales is defined as the opposite of the category of frames.
Open or closed inclusions are usually considered for maps between locales. The following are the translations to frames.
Let
f:P\to Q
be a frame morphism between posets.

Definition 4.6
.

1.

f:P\to Q
is a closed subframe inclusion if there exists
p_{f}
in
P

such that
f
restricts to a frame isomorphism
P_{p_{f}/}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}Q
.

2.

f:P\to Q
is an open subframe inclusion if there exists
p_{f}
in
P
such that

f
restricts to a frame isomorphism
P_{/p_{f}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}Q
.

Remark 4.7
.

Note that in these definitions,
Q
is the subframe of
P
.

If
P
and
Q
are the frames of open subsets of topological spaces, then
the notions of an open or closed inclusion become the usual ones.

The map between frames induced by a proper morphism between locally compact Hausdorff spaces
is an example of a perfect map.
∎

The proofs of the following two lemmas are straightforward.

Lemma 4.8
.

An open subframe inclusion
f:P\to Q
fits into a right Bousfield localization

f^{\flat}:Q\leftrightarrows P:f

(4.5)

in
\mathbf{Poset}^{\mathrm{pfr}}
,
where
ff^{\flat}(q)=q
and
f^{\flat}
is a partial frame morphism.

Corollary 4.9
.

A closed subframe inclusion
f:P\to Q

fits into a left
Bousfield localization

f:P\leftrightarrows Q:f_{\sharp}

in
\mathbf{Poset}^{\mathrm{afr}}
,
where
f_{\sharp}
is determined by
f_{\sharp}(f(p))=p\vee p_{f}
and is an almost frame morphism.

4.3
Canonical descent for
E
-theory of locales

We consider the functor

(-)C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathbf{Locale}\to\mathcal{EC}\ ,\quad X\mapsto XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ ,\quad f\mapsto f_{!}

from
3.26
.
Recall that for a locale
X
, we denote by
\mathcal{P}(X)
the same poset considered as a frame.

A map
j:U\to X
of locales is an open sublocale inclusion if
\mathcal{P}(j):\mathcal{P}(X)\to\mathcal{P}(U)
is an open subframe inclusion as described in
4.6
.
2
.

Corollary 4.10
.

If
j:U\to X
is an open sublocale inclusion, then we have a right Bousfield localization

j_{!}:UC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\leftrightarrows XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:j^{*}

and the right-adjoint
j^{*}
is a morphism between
E
-theory contexts.

Proof.

Here
j^{*}=\mathcal{P}(j)^{\flat,*}
with
\mathcal{P}(j)^{\flat}
from
4.8
and
\mathcal{P}(j)
is a partial frame morphism.
We now use the fact, that the functor to
E
-theory contexts
(-)^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is defined on
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{pfr}}
by
3.24
.
∎

A map
i:Z\to X
is a closed sublocale inclusion if
\mathcal{P}(i):\mathcal{P}(X)\to\mathcal{P}(Z)
is a closed subframe inclusion as described in
4.6
.
1
.

Corollary 4.11
.

If
i:Z\to X
is a closed sublocale inclusion, then we have a left Bousfield localization

i^{*}:XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\leftrightarrows ZC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:i_{*}:=i_{!}

and the left-adjoint
i^{*}
is a morphism between
E
-theory contexts.

Proof.

Here
i^{*}:=\mathcal{P}(i)_{\sharp}^{*}
, where
\mathcal{P}(i)_{\sharp}
from
4.9
is an almost frame morphism, and we use the fact, that the functor to
E
-theory contexts
(-)^{\mathrm{c}}C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is defined on
\mathbf{Poset}_{(*)}^{\mathrm{afr}}
by
4.2
.
∎

Let
X
be a locale and
j:U\to X
be an open sublocale inclusion corresponding to the frame morphism

\mathcal{P}(j):P\to P_{/p}\ ,\quad p^{\prime}\mapsto p\wedge p^{\prime}

for some uniquely determined
p
in
P
. The map
i:Z\to X
given by the frame morphism

\mathcal{P}(i):P\to P_{p/}\ ,\quad p^{\prime}\mapsto p\vee p^{\prime}

is then called the complementary closed sublocale inclusion.

Recall the
E
-theory functor

E:\mathbf{Locale}\to\mathbf{Pr}_{\mathrm{st}}^{L}

from
3.27
.

Theorem 4.12
.

For an open sublocale
j:U\to X
and its complementary closed sublocale
i:Z\to X

we have a recollement

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 14.82707pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.82707pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 28.7588pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.63542pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{j_{!}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 48.9936pt\raise 7.94266pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.48993pt\raise-16.61229pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.63542pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{j_{*}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 48.90755pt\raise-8.059pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 38.82707pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces E(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 20.16786pt\raise 6.35265pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.99155pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{j^{*}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.82707pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 75.62569pt\raise 16.61229pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\perp}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.74576pt\raise 28.43437pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.67209pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i^{*}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 103.05345pt\raise 7.94266pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 75.62569pt\raise-16.61229pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\perp}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 75.13695pt\raise-29.68784pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.56447pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{j^{!}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 102.96767pt\raise-8.059pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 93.63261pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces E(Z)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.74574pt\raise-5.81596pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.81596pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i_{*}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.63261pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

Proof.

The left Bousfield localization
j_{!}\dashv j^{*}
from
4.10
and the right Bousfield localization

i^{*}\dashv i_{*}
from
4.11
induce corresponding localizations after applying the
E
-theory functor. The additional right-adjoints
j_{*}
and
i^{!}
exist since
E
takes values in
\mathbf{Pr}_{\mathrm{st}}^{L}
and sends partial or almost frame morphisms to
left-adjoint functors.

For every
A
in
XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
we have an exact sequence

0\to j_{!}j^{*}A\to A\to i_{*}i^{*}A\to 0\ .

Indeed, let
p^{\prime}
be in
P
.
Then this sequence evaluates to

0\to A(p\wedge p^{\prime})\to A(p^{\prime})\to A(p\vee p^{\prime})/A(p)\to 0

which is exact, since

A(p\wedge p^{\prime})=A(p)\cap A(p^{\prime})
and
A(p\vee p^{\prime})=A(p)+A(p^{\prime})
.
The functors

\Phi:A\mapsto\mathrm{e}_{X}(j_{!}j^{*}A)

and

\Psi:A\mapsto{\mathrm{Fib}}(\mathrm{e}_{X}(A)\to\mathrm{e}_{X}(i_{*}i^{*}A))

from
XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
to
E(X)
are both homological. The exactness of
\mathrm{e}_{X}

implies an equivalence

\Phi\to\Psi
.
By the universal property of
\mathrm{e}_{X}
this equivalence
extends to an equivalence on the level of
E
-theory. More precisely, we have a fibre sequence
of endofunctors

j_{!}j^{*}\to{\mathtt{id}}\to i_{*}i^{*}

(4.6)

of
E(X)
.
∎

Theorem 4.13
.

Let
X
be a locale and
(U_{i})_{i\in I}
be a filtered system of open sublocales such that
X=\bigcup_{i\in I}U_{i}
. Then, we have an equivalence

E(X)\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{i\in I}E(U_{i})\ .

Proof.

Let
j_{i}:U_{i}\to X
denote the inclusions. We are going to check that for every
A
in
XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
we have

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}j_{i,!}j_{i}^{*}A\cong A\ .

(4.7)

Let
P=\mathcal{P}(X)
be the frame corresponding to the locale
X
.
For
p
in
P
we have

(j_{i,!}j_{i}^{*}A)(p)=A(p\wedge p_{i})
, where
p_{i}
is determined by
U_{i}
.
The condition
X=\bigcup_{i\in I}U_{i}
translates to
\bigvee_{i\in I}p_{i}=\infty_{\mathcal{P}(X)}
.
Then

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}j_{i,!}j_{i}^{*}A(p)\cong\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}A(p\wedge p_{i})\cong A(\bigvee_{i\in I}(p\wedge p_{i}))\cong A(p\wedge\bigvee_{i\in I}p_{i})\cong A(p)

using the continuity of
A
and distributivity of
P
.
Since
\mathrm{e}_{X}
preserves colimits the equivalence (
4.7
) induces an equivalence of functors

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}j_{i,!}j_{i}^{*}\mathrm{e}_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathrm{e}_{X}:XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to E(X)\ .

By the universal property of
\mathrm{e}_{X}

this equivalence extends to
an equivalence of endofunctors

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I\ }j_{i,!}j_{i}^{*}\simeq{\mathtt{id}}

(4.8)

of
E(X)
.
We conclude that the functor

E(X)\to\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{i\in I}E(U_{i})

(4.9)

is fully faithful. We now show that it is also essentially surjective.
For
(A_{i})_{i\in I}
in
\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{i\in I}E(U_{i})

we set

A:=\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i\in I}j_{i,!}A_{i}\ .

We then check that

j_{k}^{*}A\simeq A_{k}
for all
k
in
I
.
To this end, we use the fact
j_{k}^{*}
preserves colimits and the
equivalences
j_{k}^{*}j_{i,!}A_{i}\simeq j_{U_{k}\to U_{i}}^{*}A_{i}\simeq A_{k}
provided that
k\leq i
.
∎

4.4
Comparison with cosheaves

The this section we relate the category
E(X)
for a locale
X
with the
\mathrm{E}
-valued cosheaves on
X
. Under finiteness assumptions on
X
we get an equivalence. The main result is
4.22
.

Let
X
be a locale and let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a presentable stable
\infty
-category.

Definition 4.14
.

A cosheaf on
X
with values in
{\mathcal{C}}
is
a functor
F:\mathcal{P}(X)\to{\mathcal{C}}
such that:

1.

F(\emptyset)=0

2.

For every
p,q
in
\mathcal{P}(X)
, we have a pushout

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 21.28122pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-21.28122pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F(p\wedge q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 53.24818pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 53.24818pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F(p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.56244pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-13.21008pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F(q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.28122pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 45.28122pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F(p\vee q)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

3.

For every filtered family
(p_{i})_{i}
in
\mathcal{P}(X)
, we
have an equivalence

\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i}F(p_{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}F(\vee_{i}p_{i})\ .

We let

\mathrm{CoShv}(X,{\mathcal{C}})
denote the stable
\infty
-category of cosheaves on
X
.

A morphism of locales
f:X\to Y
gives rise to a colimit preserving functor

f_{!}:\mathrm{CoShv}(X,{\mathcal{C}})\to\mathrm{CoShv}(Y,{\mathcal{C}})

by restricting the functor
{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathcal{P}(X),{\mathcal{C}})\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathcal{P}(Y),{\mathcal{C}})
induced by the morphism

\mathcal{P}(f):\mathcal{P}(Y)\to\mathcal{P}(X)
. In the case of an open inclusion, it is fully faithful and strongly cocontinuous and has two further right-adjoints
f^{*}
and
f_{*}
. In the case of a closed inclusion, it is fully faithful and has a further left-adjoint
f^{*}
and a right-adjoint
f^{!}
, and we set
f_{!}=f_{*}
.
These facts can be seen similarly to
4.10
and
4.11
.

We recall the following well-known fact.

Proposition 4.15
.

Assume that
j:U\to X
and
i:Z\to X
are complementary inclusions of an open and a closed sublocale.
Then we have a recollement

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 29.45906pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-29.45906pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 75.68286pt\raise 7.94266pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 75.54993pt\raise-8.059pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise Proof.

The proof is very similar to the one for
Theorem

4.12
.
We again use the fibre sequences for cosheaves
F

j_{!}j^{*}F\to F\to i_{*}i^{*}F\ .

∎

We further recall the following well-known continuity property of cosheaves.

Proposition 4.16
.

Let
X
be a locale and
(U_{i})_{i\in I}
be a filtered system of open sublocales such that
X=\bigcup_{i\in I}U
. Then we have an equivalence

\mathrm{CoShv}(P,\mathrm{E})\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{lim}}_{i\in I\ }\mathrm{CoShv}(U_{i},\mathrm{E})\ .

Proof.

This has the same proof as
Theorem

4.13
starting with the observation that (
4.8
) holds for cosheaves.
∎

Lemma 4.17
.

There exists a canonical natural transformation of functors

s:E\to{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathcal{P}(-),\mathrm{E}):\mathbf{Locale}\to\mathbf{Pr}_{\mathrm{st}}^{L}

(4.10)

which sends
A
in
E(X)
to the cosheaf
s_{X}(A):\mathcal{P}(X)\ni p\mapsto\mathrm{e}(A(p))
.

Proof.

Let
X
be a locale. Since the right-down composition in

\textstyle{XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}_{X}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathcal{P}(X),C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{e}\circ-}
\textstyle{E(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s_{X}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{Fun}}(\mathcal{P}(X),\mathrm{E})}

is homological, we get the colimit-preserving factorization
s_{X}
.
Applying the same argument to the whole diagram over
\mathbf{Locale}
we get the
transformation of functors (
4.10
).
∎

Let
X
be a locale.

Theorem 4.18
.

1.

The functor

s_{X}
corestricts to a colimit-preserving functor

s_{X}:E(X)\to\mathrm{CoShv}(X,\mathrm{E})\ .

2.

If
X
is finite, then
s_{X}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

If
A
is in
XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
, then
\mathrm{e}(A(\emptyset))\simeq\mathrm{e}(0)\simeq 0
.
Since
A
is continuous, for
p
and
q
in
\mathcal{P}(X)
we have, using the exactness of
\mathrm{e}:C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\to\mathrm{E}
, a pushout

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 27.23262pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-27.23262pt\raise q))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.19958pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{e}(A(p))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 78.46524pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-19.16148pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 51.23262pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 51.23262pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{e}(A(p\vee q))}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\ .

Similarly, using the facts that
\mathrm{e}
preserves filtered colimits and that
A
is continuous,
for a filtered family
(p_{i})_{i}
in
\mathcal{P}(X)
we have

\mathrm{e}(A(\vee_{i}p_{i}))\simeq\operatorname*{\mathtt{colim}}_{i}\mathrm{e}(A(p_{i}))\ .

These relations show that
s_{X}(A)
is a cosheaf for every
A
in
XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
.
Using the universal property of
\mathrm{e}_{X}
these
relations extend to all objects of
E(X)
. We conclude that
s_{X}
takes values in cosheaves.
This finishes the verification of Assertion
1
.

We now show Assertion
2
by induction on the number of elements of
P
. The start of the induction is at the locale corresponding to the frame
[1]
, where the assertion is obvious.

Consider now a finite locale
X
.
Let
p
be a minimal non-minimal element of
\mathcal{P}(X)
. It determines an open sublocale inclusion
U\to X
.
We let
Z\to X
be the closed complement.

Combining
4.15
and
Theorem

4.12

we have a map of cofibre sequences in
{\mathbf{Cat}}^{\mathrm{ex}}_{\infty}

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 29.25073pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.82707pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 67.95218pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern By the induction hypothesis, the outer vertical maps are equivalences as indicated.
This implies that the middle map is an equivalence.
∎

Remark 4.19
.

The problem of calculating mapping groups in the
KK
or
E
-theory of
XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
for finite locales using methods from triangulated category theory
has been considered in

[
MN08
]
,
[
MN09
]
,
[
Ben13
]
. In the case of
E
-theory the following provides a complete homotopy theoretic description of the whole mapping space.

Corollary 4.20
.

If
X
is finite, then
for
A,B
in
E(X)
we have

{\mathtt{map}}_{E(X)}(A,B)\simeq{\mathtt{map}}_{\mathrm{CoShv}(X,\mathrm{E})}(s(A),s(B))\ .

∎

Example 4.21
.

Every
finite topological space
X
gives rise to a finite locale
\mathrm{Open}(X)
.
Therefore
Theorem

4.18
and
4.20
apply to
X
-
C^{*}
-algebras for finite topological spaces.
∎

Combining
4.16
,
Theorem

4.13
with
Theorem

4.18
we get.

Corollary 4.22
.

Let
X
be a locale which is a union of a filtered family of
finite open sublocales; then we have an equivalence

s_{X}:E(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\to}}\mathrm{CoShv}(X,\mathrm{E})\ .

Example 4.23
.

The locale corresponding to the poset
{\rm Nature}
satisfies the assumption of
4.22
.
∎

5
Locally compact Hausdorff spaces

5.1
Statement of the main theorem

Let
\mathrm{LCH}
denote the category of locally compact Hausdorff spaces.
By restriction along
\mathrm{LCH}\to\mathbf{Locale}
, the functor from
3.26
gives rise to a functor

(-)C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathrm{LCH}\to\mathcal{EC}\ ,\quad X\mapsto XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ ,\quad f\mapsto f_{!}\ .

In order to construct a six-functor formalism, we need a contravariant functor.

Theorem 5.1
.

There is a lax symmetric monoidal functor into
E
-theory contexts

(-)C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathrm{LCH}^{\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}\ ,\quad X\mapsto XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}\ ,\quad f\mapsto f^{*}\ .

The proof of this theorem will be given in
Section

5.9
.
The interaction between the covariant functoriality with the contravariant functoriality and the tensor product
will be formulated as follows:

Theorem 5.2
.

The lax symmetric monoidal functor

(-)C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}:\mathrm{LCH}^{\mathrm{op}}\to{\mathbf{Cat}}

(5.1)

from
Theorem

5.1

is a three-functor formalism in the sense of
6.7
.

The proof of this theorem is given in
Section

5.7
.

5.2
Equivalent pictures of
XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}

Let
X
be a locally compact Hausdorff space and recall that
XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
is defined, by combining
3.26
and
3.22
, as the category of pairs

(A,A(-):\mathrm{Open}(X)\to I(A))

such that
A(-)
is a frame morphism.

Example 5.3
.

We have the preferred object
C_{0}(X)_{X}
in
XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
. Its underlying
C^{*}
-algebra is
C_{0}(X)
and the corresponding frame map

\mathrm{Open}(X)\to I(C_{0}(X))
sends
U
to the ideal

C_{0}(U)
.
∎

The following proposition states a fact that is well-known in the literature on
C_{0}(X)
-algebras and essentially stated in
[
MN09
]
. Since it is crucial for the understanding of the categorical properties of

XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
for locally compact Hausdorff spaces shown in
Theorem

5.15
,
we give the argument as a service to the reader.

Proposition 5.4
.

For a locally compact Hausdorff space
X
, providing an
X
-
C^{*}
-algebra
structure on
A
is equivalent to providing a pair
(A,m)
of a
C^{*}
-algebra
A
together with
a surjective
A
-bilinear homomorphism

m:C_{0}(X)\otimes A\to A
.

Proof.

Recall the topological space
\mathrm{Prim}(A)
from
3.29
.
In this proof, we will use the following constructions and facts:
Let
A
be in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}

and
\rho:A\to B(H)
be in
\mathrm{Prim}(A)
. Then
\rho(A)^{\prime}\cong{\mathbb{C}}
, where
\rho(A)^{\prime}
is the commutant of
\rho(A)
in
B(H)
.
Since
\rho
is irreducible, it is essential in the sense that
\rho(A)H
is dense in
H
. The representation
\rho
therefore has a unique extension
\hat{\rho}:M(A)\to B(H)
to the multiplier algebra
M(A)
of
A
.
We get a homomorphism

Z(M(A))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hat{\rho}}}{{\to}}\rho(A)^{\prime}\cong{\mathbb{C}}\ ,

where
Z(M(A))
denotes the center of
M(A)
.
This induces a homomorphism

Z(M(A))\to C_{b}(\mathrm{Prim}(A))\ ,\quad z\mapsto(\rho\mapsto\hat{\rho}(z))\ .

(5.2)

We collect the following facts for later reference.

Fact 5.5
.

1.

The Dauns-Hofmann theorem states that the homomorphism in (
5.2
) is an isomorphism.

2.

For every
a
in
A
we have
\|a\|=\sup_{\rho\in\mathrm{Prim}(A)}\|\rho(a)\|

[
Dix83
, 3.3.6]
.

3.

For
a
in
A
and
r
in
(0,\infty)
the subset
\{\rho\in\mathrm{Prim}(A)|\rho(a)\geq r\}

is (quasi)compact
[
Dix83
, 3.3.7]
.

Lemma 5.6

(
[
MN09
, Lem.2.25]
)
.

For every sober topological space,
the data of an
X
-
C^{*}
-algebra structure on
A
is equivalent to the data of a map
\tilde{\psi}:\mathrm{Prim}(A)\to X
of topological spaces.

Proof.

By definition, an
X
-
C^{*}
-algebra is a frame morphism
A:\mathrm{Open}(X)\to I(A)
.
We have
I(A)\cong\mathrm{Open}(\mathrm{Prim}(A))
; hence,
this map is the same as a frame morphism

\mathrm{Open}(X)\to\mathrm{Open}(\mathrm{Prim}(A))
.
Since
X
is sober, the data of such a map is the same as a continuous map
\tilde{\psi}:\mathrm{Prim}(A)\to X
of topological spaces.
∎

Lemma 5.7

(
[
MN09
, Sec. 2.1]
)
.

If
X
is locally compact Hausdorff, then the data of a continuous map
\tilde{\psi}:\mathrm{Prim}(A)\to X
of topological spaces
is equivalent the data of an essential homomorphism

C_{0}(X)\to Z(M(A))
.

Proof.

The map
\tilde{\psi}
gives map
\psi:C_{0}(X)\to C_{b}(\mathrm{Prim}(A))\cong Z(M(A))
. In order to see that
it is essential, let
b
be in
Z(M(A))
and assume that
\psi(f)b=0
for all
f
in
C_{0}(X)
.
Let
\rho:A\to B(H)
be in
\mathrm{Prim}(A)
and consider
x:=\tilde{\psi}(b)
in
X
.
We then have
\hat{\rho}(\psi(f)b)=f(x)\hat{\rho}(b)
.
Since
X
is locally compact Hausdorff, we can find
f
in
C_{0}(X)
with
f(x)\not=0
.
We can therefore conclude that
\hat{\rho}(b)=0
for all
\rho
in
\mathrm{Prim}(A)
. This implies
b=0
by the injectivity part of the Dauns-Hofmann theorem
5.5
.
1
.

Concersely, assume that
\psi:C_{0}(X)\to Z(M(A))
is an essential homomorphism.
Then we get a continuous map
\tilde{\psi}:\mathrm{Prim}(A)\to X
. Its Gelfand dual is the composition

\tilde{\psi}(\rho):C_{0}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi}}{{\to}}Z(M(A))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hat{\rho}}}{{\to}}\rho(A)^{\prime}\cong{\mathbb{C}}\ .

This homomorphism is indeed non-zero since
\psi
is essential. ∎

The following lemma is essentially explained in
[
MN09
]
.

Lemma 5.8
.

If
X
is a locally compact Hausdorff,
then essential homomorphisms
\psi:C_{0}(X)\to Z(M(A))
are in bijection with surjective
A
-bilinear homomorphisms

m:C_{0}(X)\otimes A\to A
.

Proof.

Given
\psi
we get a homomorphism

C_{0}(X)\otimes^{\mathrm{alg}}A\to A
which induces
m
by the universal property of the tensor product.
We first show that
m
is essential. Let
a
be in
A
and assume that
m(f\otimes b)a=0
for all
f
in
C_{0}(X)
and
b
in
A
. Then
b\psi(f)a=0
for all
b
in
A
and hence
\psi(f)a=0
for all
f
. This gives

\sup_{\rho\in\mathrm{Prim}(A)}\|f(\tilde{\psi}(\rho))\rho(a)\|=0\ .

Since again for every
x
in
X
(so in particular for
x=\tilde{\psi}(\rho)
) there exists a function
f
in
C_{0}(X)
with
f(x)\not=0
we conclude that

\sup_{\rho\in\mathrm{Prim}(A)}\|\rho(a)\|=0
which implies
a=0
by
5.5
.
2
.

We now show that

m:C_{0}(X)\otimes A\to A
is surjective.
It suffices to show that
m
has a dense image. Let
a
be in
A
and fix
\epsilon
in
(0,\infty)
.
Then
K:=\{\rho\in\mathrm{Prim}(A)\mid\|\rho(a)\|\geq\epsilon\}
is compact by
5.5
.
3
.
Hence
\tilde{\psi}(K)
is a compact subset of
X
. Using that
X
is locally compact Hausdorff, we can
choose
f
in
C_{0}(X)
such that
\|f\|\leq 1
and
f_{|K}\equiv 1
.
We then have

\|a-m(f\otimes a)\|=\sup_{\rho\in\mathrm{Prim}(A)}\|\rho(a-\psi(f)a)\|=\sup_{\rho\in\mathrm{Prim}(A)}\|(1-f(\tilde{\psi}(\rho))\rho(a)\|\leq\epsilon\ .

Vice versa, a surjective
A
-bilinear homomorphism
m:C_{0}(X)\otimes A\to A
induces a homomorphism

\psi:C_{0}(X)\to Z(M(A))
by
\psi(f)a:=m(f\otimes a)
. The central multiplier property follows from
A
-bilinearity of
m
by

\psi(f)(ab)=m(f\otimes ab)=m(f\otimes a)b=\psi(f)(a)b
and
\psi(f)(ab)=m(f\otimes ab)=am(f\otimes b)=a\psi(f)(b)
.
We must check that
\psi
is essential.
Let
z
be in
Z(M(A))
and assume that
\psi(f)z=0
for all
f
in
C_{0}(X)
. We must show that
z=0
. The assumption implies that

\psi(f)zab=\psi(f)azb=m(f\otimes a)zb=0
for all
f
in
C_{0}(X)
and
a,b
in
A
.
Since
m
is surjective, we conclude that
zb=0
for all
b
in
A
, and thus
z=0
.
∎

The combination of
5.6
,
5.7
, and
5.8

proves
5.4
.
∎

Remark 5.9
.

In view of
5.8
and
5.4
,
our definition of
XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
for a locally compact Hausdorff space
X
coincides with
[
MN09
, Def. 2.1]
.
The combination of
5.7
and
5.6
furthermore shows that,
in this case, our definition of
XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
coincides with the standard definition of a
C_{0}(X)
-algebra as an algebra
A
equipped
with an essential homomorphism
C_{0}(X)\to Z(M(A))
.
By
5.6
, our definition of
XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
for a sober topological space
X
coincides with

[
MN09
, Def. 2.3]
.
∎

Remark 5.10
.

In view of
5.4

we consider objects of
XC^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
equivalently as pairs
(A,m)

of a
C^{*}
-algebra and an
A
-bilinear surjective homomorphism
m:C_{0}(X)\otimes A\to A
. In this picture, the value of the functor
A:\mathrm{Open}(X)\to I(A)

is given by

A(U)={\mathtt{im}}(m:C_{0}(U)\otimes A\to A)\ .

(5.3)

A morphism
f:(A,m)\to(A^{\prime},m^{\prime})
is then a homomorphism
f:A\to A^{\prime}

in
C^{*}\mathbf{Alg}^{\mathrm{nu}}
such that the following diagram commutes:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 27.459pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-27.459pt\raise
